This is a translation layer to allow\n292\t// us to use (and receive) the standard serialization format.\n293\t// See section 5.4.9.2 of https://zips.z.cash/protocol/protocol.pdf\n294\t// and https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-irtf-cfrg-bls-signature/ Appendix A\n295\t// for its choice as a standard.\n296\t\n297\t// A note on BLST: blst uses Zcash point encoding formats across the board.\n298\t\n299\tfn bls_fq_to_zcash_bytes(field: &Fq) -> [u8; G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE] {\n300\t    let mut result = [0u8; G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE];\n301\t\n302\t    let rep = field.into_repr();\n303\t\n304\t    result[0..8].copy_from_slice(&rep.0[5].to_be_bytes());\n305\t    result[8..16].copy_from_slice(&rep.0[4].to_be_bytes());\n306\t    result[16..24].copy_from_slice(&rep.0[3].to_be_bytes());\n307\t    result[24..32].copy_from_slice(&rep.0[2].to_be_bytes());\n308\t    result[32..40].copy_from_slice(&rep.0[1].to_be_bytes());\n309\t    result[40..48].copy_from_slice(&rep.0[0].to_be_bytes());\n310\t\n311\t    result\n312\t}\n313\t\n314\tfn bls_fq_from_zcash_bytes(bytes: &[u8; G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE]) -> Option<Fq> {\n315\t    let mut tmp = BigInteger384::default();\n316\t\n317\t    tmp.0[5] = u64::from_be_bytes(bytes[0..8].try_into().unwrap());\n318\t    tmp.0[4] = u64::from_be_bytes(bytes[8..16].try_into().unwrap());\n319\t    tmp.0[3] = u64::from_be_bytes(bytes[16..24].try_into().unwrap());\n320\t    tmp.0[2] = u64::from_be_bytes(bytes[24..32].try_into().unwrap());\n321\t    tmp.0[1] = u64::from_be_bytes(bytes[32..40].try_into().unwrap());\n322\t    tmp.0[0] = u64::from_be_bytes(bytes[40..48].try_into().unwrap());\n323\t\n324\t    Fq::from_repr(tmp)\n325\t}\n326\t\n327\tstruct EncodingFlags {\n328\t    is_compressed: bool,\n329\t    is_infinity: bool,\n330\t    is_lexicographically_largest: bool,\n331\t}\n332\t\n333\timpl From<&[u8]> for EncodingFlags {\n334\t    fn from(bytes: &[u8]) -> Self {\n335\t        Self {\n336\t            is_compressed: ((bytes[0] >> 7) & 1) == 1,\n337\t            is_infinity: (bytes[0] >> 6) & 1 == 1,\n338\t            is_lexicographically_largest: (bytes[0] >> 5) & 1 == 1,\n339\t        }\n340\t    }\n341\t}\n342\t\n343\timpl EncodingFlags {\n344\t    fn encode_flags(&self, bytes: &mut [u8]) {\n345\t        if self.is_compressed {\n346\t            bytes[0] |= 1 << 7;\n347\t        }\n348\t\n349\t        if self.is_infinity {\n350\t            bytes[0] |= 1 << 6;\n351\t        }\n352\t\n353\t        if self.is_compressed && !self.is_infinity && self.is_lexicographically_largest {\n354\t            bytes[0] |= 1 << 5;\n355\t        }\n356\t    }\n357\t}\n358\t\n359\t/// This deserializes an Arkworks G1Affine point from a Zcash point encoding.\n360\tpub fn bls_g1_affine_from_zcash_bytes(bytes: &[u8; G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE]) -> Option<BlsG1Affine> {\n361\t    // Obtain the three flags from the start of the byte sequence.\n362\t    let flags = EncodingFlags::from(&bytes[..]);\n363\t\n364\t    if !flags.is_compressed {\n365\t        return None; // We only support compressed points.\n366\t    }\n367\t\n368\t    if flags.is_infinity {\n369\t        return Some(BlsG1Affine::default());\n370\t    }\n371\t    // Attempt to obtain the x-coordinate.\n372\t    let x = obtain_x_coordinate(bytes.as_slice())?;\n373\t\n374\t    BlsG1Affine::get_point_from_x(x, flags.is_lexicographically_largest)\n375\t}\n376\t\n377\t/// This serializes an Arkworks G1Affine point into a Zcash point encoding.\n378\tpub fn bls_g1_affine_to_zcash_bytes(p: &BlsG1Affine) -> [u8; G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE] {\n379\t    let mut result = bls_fq_to_zcash_bytes(&p.x);\n380\t    let encoding = EncodingFlags {\n381\t        is_compressed: true,\n382\t        is_infinity: p.infinity,\n383\t        is_lexicographically_largest: p.y > -p.y,\n384\t    };\n385\t    encoding.encode_flags(&mut result[..]);\n386\t    result\n387\t}\n388\t\n389\t/// This deserializes an Arkworks G2Affine point from a Zcash point encoding.\n390\tpub fn bls_g2_affine_from_zcash_bytes(bytes: &[u8; G2_COMPRESSED_SIZE]) -> Option<BlsG2Affine> {\n391\t    // Obtain the three flags from the start of the byte sequence\n392\t    let flags = EncodingFlags::from(&bytes[..]);\n393\t\n394\t    if !flags.is_compressed {\n395\t        return None; // We only support compressed points.\n396\t    }\n397\t\n398\t    if flags.is_infinity {\n399\t        return Some(BlsG2Affine::default());\n400\t    }\n401\t\n402\t    let xc1 = obtain_x_coordinate(bytes.as_slice())?;\n403\t    let xc0 = {\n404\t        let mut tmp = [0; G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE];\n405\t        tmp.copy_from_slice(&bytes[48..96]);\n406\t\n407\t        bls_fq_from_zcash_bytes(&tmp)?\n408\t    };\n409\t\n410\t    let x = Fq2::new(xc0, xc1);\n411\t\n412\t    BlsG2Affine::get_point_from_x(x, flags.is_lexicographically_largest)\n413\t}\n414\t\n415\t/// This serializes an Arkworks G2Affine point into a Zcash point encoding.\n416\tpub fn bls_g2_affine_to_zcash_bytes(p: &BlsG2Affine) -> [u8; G2_COMPRESSED_SIZE] {\n417\t    let mut bytes = [0u8; G2_COMPRESSED_SIZE];\n418\t\n419\t    let c1_bytes = bls_fq_to_zcash_bytes(&p.x.c1);\n420\t    let c0_bytes = bls_fq_to_zcash_bytes(&p.x.c0);\n421\t    bytes[0..48].copy_from_slice(&c1_bytes[..]);\n422\t    bytes[48..96].copy_from_slice(&c0_bytes[..]);\n423\t\n424\t    let encoding = EncodingFlags {\n425\t        is_compressed: true,\n426\t        is_infinity: p.infinity,\n427\t        is_lexicographically_largest: p.y > -p.y,\n428\t    };\n429\t\n430\t    encoding.encode_flags(&mut bytes[..]);\n431\t    bytes\n432\t}\n433\t\n434\t// Attempt to obtain x_coordinate\n435\tfn obtain_x_coordinate(bytes: &[u8]) -> Option<Fq> {\n436\t    let mut tmp = [0; G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE];\n437\t    // this is safe as the private obtain_x_coordinate function is only invoked with G1 (48) and\n438\t    // G2 (96) size inputs.\n439\t    tmp.copy_from_slice(&bytes[0..G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE]);\n440\t\n441\t    // Mask away the flag bits\n442\t    tmp[0] &= 0b0001_1111;\n443\t\n444\t    bls_fq_from_zcash_bytes(&tmp)\n445\t}\n446\t\n447\t#[cfg(test)]\n448\tpub(crate) mod tests {\n449\t    use super::*;\n450\t    use blst_uint64_from_fr};\nuse byte_slice_cast::AsByteSlice;\n\nconst SCALAR_SIZE: usize = 32;\n/// G1 affine point compressed size.\npub const G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE: usize = 48;\nconst G2_COMPRESSED_SIZE: usize = 96;\n\nconst G1_UNCOMPRESSED_SIZE: usize = 96;\nconst G2_UNCOMPRESSED_SIZE: usize = 192;\n\n#[inline]\nfn u64s_from_bytes(bytes: &[u8; 32]) -> [u64; 4] {\n    [\n        u64::from_le_bytes(bytes[0..8].try_into().unwrap()),\n        u64::from_le_bytes(bytes[8..16].try_into().unwrap()),\n        u64::from_le_bytes(bytes[16..24].try_into().unwrap()),\n        u64::from_le_bytes(bytes[24..32].try_into().unwrap()),\n    ]\n}\n\n// Scalar Field conversions\n/// Convert an Arkworks BLS12-381 scalar field element to a blst scalar field element.\npub fn bls_fr_to_blst_fr(fe: &BlsFr) -> blst_fr {\n    debug_assert_eq!(fe.serialized_size(), SCALAR_SIZE);\n    let mut bytes = [0u8; SCALAR_SIZE];\n    fe.serialize_with_flags(&mut bytes[..], Convert a blst scalar field element to an Arkworks BLS12-381 scalar field element.\npub fn blst_fr_to_bls_fr(fe: &blst_fr) -> BlsFr {\n    let mut out = [0u64; 4];\n    unsafe { extension field element to a blst quadratic extension\n/// field element.\npub fn bls_fq2_to_blst_fp2(f: &Fq2) -> blst_fp2 {\n    let mut fp_bytes_le = [0u8; G2_UNCOMPRESSED_SIZE / 2];\n    f.serialize_uncompressed(&mut fp_bytes_le[..])\n        .expect(\"fp size correct\");\n\n    blst_fp2 {\n        fp: fp_bytes_le\n            .chunks(48)\n            .map(|fp_bytes| {\n  Fq2::new(bls_fp1, bls_fp2)\n}\n\n// Target Field conversions\n\n/// Convert an Arkworks BLS12-381 degree-6 extension field element to a blst degree-6 extension\n/// field element.\npub fn field element to a blst target field element.\npub fn bls_fq12_to_blst_fp12(f: &Fq12) -> blst_fp12 {\n    let c0 = bls_fq6_to_blst_fp6(&f.c0);\n    let c1 = bls_fq6_to_blst_fp6(&f.c1);\n    blst_fp12 provided in affine form.\n\nfn blst_g1_affine_infinity() -> blst_p1_affine {\n    blst_p1_affine {\n        x: blst_fp::default(),\n        y: blst_fp::default(),\n    }\n}\n\nfn bls_g1_affine_infinity() -> BlsG1Affine {\n    BlsG1Affine::new(Fq::zero(), Fq::one(), true)\n}\n\n/// Convert an Arkworks BLS12-381 affine G1 point to a blst affine G1 point.\npub fn bls_g1_affine_to_blst_g1_affine(pt: &BlsG1Affine) -> blst_p1_affine {\n    debug_assert_eq!(pt.uncompressed_size(), G1_UNCOMPRESSED_SIZE);\n    // BLST disagrees with Arkworks on the uncompressed representation of the infinity point on G1\n    // https://github.com/supranational/blst/blob/6382d67c72119d563975892ed49ba32e92d3d0da/src/e1.c#L153-L162\n    // the infinity bit notwithstanding,\n    // Arkworks: x = Fq::zero(), y = Fq::one()\n    // BLST: x = y = Fq::zero()\n    // BLST follows the standard here (see https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-irtf-cfrg-pairing-friendly-curves-10#appendix-C)\n    if pt.infinity {\n        return blst_g1_affine_infinity();\n    }\n    let tmp_p1 = blst_p1_affine {\n        x: bls_fq_to_blst_fp(&pt.x),\n        y: bls_fq_to_blst_fp(&pt.y),\n    };\n    // See https://github.com/arkworks-rs/curves/issues/14 for why the double serialize\n    // we're in fact applying correct masks that arkworks does not use. This may be solved\n    // alternatively using https://github.com/arkworks-rs/algebra/issues/308 in a later release of\n    // Arkworks.\n    // Note: this is an uncompressed serialization - deserialization.\n    let mut tmp2 = [0u8; 96];\n    unsafe {\n        blst_p1_affine_serialize(tmp2.as_mut_ptr(), &tmp_p1);\n    };\n\n    let mut g1 = blst_p1_affine::default();\n    debug_assert_eq!(\n        unsafe { blst_p1_deserialize(&mut g1, tmp2.as_ptr()) },\n        BLST_ERROR::BLST_SUCCESS\n    );\n    g1\n}\n\n/// Convert a blst affine G1 point to an Arkworks BLS12-381 affine G1 point.\npub fn blst_g1_affine_to_bls_g1_affine(pt: &blst_p1_affine) -> BlsG1Affine {\n    let mut out = [0u8; G1_UNCOMPRESSED_SIZE];\n    unsafe {\n        blst_p1_affine_serialize(out.as_mut_ptr(), pt);\n    }\n    let infinity = out[0] == 0x40;\n    // BLST disagrees with Arkworks on the uncompressed representation of the infinity point on G1\n    // https://github.com/supranational/blst/blob/6382d67c72119d563975892ed49ba32e92d3d0da/src/e1.c#L153-L162\n    // the infinity bit notwithstanding,\n    // Arkworks: x = Fq::zero(), y = Fq::one()\n    // BLST: x = y = Fq::zero()\n    // BLST follows the standard here (see https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-irtf-cfrg-pairing-friendly-curves-10#appendix-C)\n    if infinity {\n        return bls_g1_affine_infinity();\n    }\n    let y = Fp384::from_be_bytes_mod_order(&out[48..]);\n    let x = {\n        // Mask away the flag bits (which Arkworks doesn't use).\n        out[0] &= 0b0001_1111;\n        Fp384::from_be_bytes_mod_order(&out[..48])\n    };\n    BlsG1Affine::new(x, y, infinity)\n}\n\nfn blst_g2_affine_infinity() -> blst_p2_affine {\n    blst_p2_affine {\n        x: blst_fp2::default(),\n        y: blst_fp2::default(),\n    }\n}\n\nfn bls_g2_affine_infinity() -> BlsG2Affine {\n    BlsG2Affine::new(Fq2::zero(), Fq2::one(), true)\n}\n\n/// Convert an Arkworks BLS12-381 affine G2 point to a blst affine G2 point.\npub fn bls_g2_affine_to_blst_g2_affine(pt: &BlsG2Affine) -> blst_p2_affine {\n    debug_assert_eq!(pt.uncompressed_size(), G2_UNCOMPRESSED_SIZE);\n    // BLST disagrees with Arkworks on the uncompressed representation of the infinity point on G2\n    // https://github.com/supranational/blst/blob/6382d67c72119d563975892ed49ba32e92d3d0da/src/e2.c#L194-L203\n    // the infinity bit notwithstanding,\n    // Arkworks: x = Fq2::zero(), y = Fq2::one()\n    // BLST: x = y = Fq2::zero()\n    // BLST follows the standard here (see https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-irtf-cfrg-pairing-friendly-curves-10#appendix-C)\n    if pt.infinity {\n        return blst_g2_affine_infinity();\n    }\n    let tmp_p2 = blst_p2_affine {\n        x: bls_fq2_to_blst_fp2(&pt.x),\n        y: bls_fq2_to_blst_fp2(&pt.y),\n    };\n    // See https://github.com/arkworks-rs/curves/issues/14 for why the double serialize\n    // we're in fact applying correct masks that arkworks does not use. This may be solved\n    // alternatively using https://github.com/arkworks-rs/algebra/issues/308 in a later release of\n    // Arkworks.\n    // Note: this is an uncompressed serialization - deserialization.\n    let mut tmp2 = [0u8; G2_UNCOMPRESSED_SIZE];\n    unsafe {\n        blst_p2_affine_serialize(tmp2.as_mut_ptr(), &tmp_p2);\n    };\n\n    let mut g2 = blst_p2_affine::default();\n    debug_assert_eq!(\n        unsafe { blst_p2_deserialize(&mut g2, tmp2.as_ptr()) },\n        BLST_ERROR::BLST_SUCCESS\n    );\n    g2\n}\n\n/// Convert a blst affine G2 point to an Arkworks BLS12-381 affine G2 point.\npub fn blst_g2_affine_to_bls_g2_affine(pt: &blst_p2_affine) -> BlsG2Affine {\n    let ptx = blst_fp2_to_bls_fq2(&pt.x);\n    let pty = blst_fp2_to_bls_fq2(&pt.y);\n\n    let infinity = ptx == Fq2::zero();\n    // BLST disagrees with Arkworks on the uncompressed representation of the infinity point on G2\n    // https://github.com/supranational/blst/blob/6382d67c72119d563975892ed49ba32e92d3d0da/src/e2.c#L194-L203\n    // the infinity bit notwithstanding,\n    // Arkworks: x = Fq2::zero(), y = Fq2::one()\n    // BLST: x = y = Fq2::zero()\n    // BLST follows the standard here (see https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/draft-irtf-cfrg-pairing-friendly-curves-10#appendix-C)\n    if infinity {\n        bls_g2_affine_infinity()\n    } else {\n        BlsG2Affine::new(ptx, pty, infinity)\n    }\n}\n\n/////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n// Zcash point encodings to Arkworks points and back       //\n/////////////////////////////////////////////////////////////\n\n// The standard for serialization of compressed G1, G2 points of BLS12-381\n// is not followed by Arkworks 0.3.0. This is a translation layer to allow\n// us to use (and receive) the standard serialization format.\n// See section 5.4.9.2 of https://zips.z.cash/protocol/protocol.pdf\n// and https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/draft-irtf-cfrg-bls-signature/ Appendix A\n// for its choice as a standard.\n\n// A note on BLST: blst uses Zcash point encoding formats across the board.\n\nfn bls_fq_to_zcash_bytes(field: &Fq) -> [u8; G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE] {\n    let mut result = [0u8; G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE];\n\n    let rep = field.into_repr();\n\n    result[0..8].copy_from_slice(&rep.0[5].to_be_bytes());\n    result[8..16].copy_from_slice(&rep.0[4].to_be_bytes());\n    result[16..24].copy_from_slice(&rep.0[3].to_be_bytes());\n    result[24..32].copy_from_slice(&rep.0[2].to_be_bytes());\n    result[32..40].copy_from_slice(&rep.0[1].to_be_bytes());\n    result[40..48].copy_from_slice(&rep.0[0].to_be_bytes());\n\n    result\n}\n\nfn bls_fq_from_zcash_bytes(bytes: &[u8; G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE]) -> Option<Fq> {\n    let mut tmp = BigInteger384::default();\n\n    tmp.0[5] = u64::from_be_bytes(bytes[0..8].try_into().unwrap());\n    tmp.0[4] = u64::from_be_bytes(bytes[8..16].try_into().unwrap());\n    tmp.0[3] = u64::from_be_bytes(bytes[16..24].try_into().unwrap());\n    tmp.0[2] = u64::from_be_bytes(bytes[24..32].try_into().unwrap());\n    tmp.0[1] = u64::from_be_bytes(bytes[32..40].try_into().unwrap());\n    tmp.0[0] = u64::from_be_bytes(bytes[40..48].try_into().unwrap());\n\n    Fq::from_repr(tmp)\n}\n\nstruct EncodingFlags {\n    is_compressed: bool,\n    is_infinity: bool,\n    is_lexicographically_largest: bool,\n}\n\nimpl From<&[u8]> for EncodingFlags {\n    fn from(bytes: &[u8]) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            is_compressed: ((bytes[0] >> 7) & 1) == 1,\n            is_infinity: (bytes[0] >> 6) & 1 == 1,\n            is_lexicographically_largest: (bytes[0] >> 5) & 1 == 1,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl EncodingFlags {\n    fn encode_flags(&self, bytes: &mut [u8]) {\n        if self.is_compressed {\n            bytes[0] |= 1 << 7;\n        }\n\n        if self.is_infinity {\n            bytes[0] |= 1 << 6;\n        }\n\n        if self.is_compressed && !self.is_infinity && self.is_lexicographically_largest {\n            bytes[0] |= 1 << 5;\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// This deserializes an Arkworks G1Affine point from a Zcash point encoding.\npub fn bls_g1_affine_from_zcash_bytes(bytes: &[u8; G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE]) -> Option<BlsG1Affine> {\n    // Obtain the three flags from the start of the byte sequence.\n    let flags = EncodingFlags::from(&bytes[..]);\n\n    if !flags.is_compressed {\n        return None; // We only support compressed points.\n    }\n\n    if flags.is_infinity {\n        return Some(BlsG1Affine::default());\n    }\n    // Attempt to obtain the x-coordinate.\n    let x = obtain_x_coordinate(bytes.as_slice())?;\n\n    BlsG1Affine::get_point_from_x(x, flags.is_lexicographically_largest)\n}\n\n/// This serializes an Arkworks G1Affine point into a Zcash point encoding.\npub fn bls_g1_affine_to_zcash_bytes(p: &BlsG1Affine) -> [u8; G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE] {\n    let mut result = bls_fq_to_zcash_bytes(&p.x);\n    let encoding = EncodingFlags {\n        is_compressed: true,\n        is_infinity: p.infinity,\n        is_lexicographically_largest: p.y > -p.y,\n    };\n    encoding.encode_flags(&mut result[..]);\n    result\n}\n\n/// This deserializes an Arkworks G2Affine point from a Zcash point encoding.\npub fn bls_g2_affine_from_zcash_bytes(bytes: &[u8; G2_COMPRESSED_SIZE]) -> Option<BlsG2Affine> {\n    // Obtain the three flags from the start of the byte sequence\n    let flags = EncodingFlags::from(&bytes[..]);\n\n    if !flags.is_compressed {\n        return None; // We only support compressed points.\n    }\n\n    if flags.is_infinity {\n        return Some(BlsG2Affine::default());\n    }\n\n    let xc1 = obtain_x_coordinate(bytes.as_slice())?;\n    let xc0 = {\n        let mut tmp = [0; G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE];\n        tmp.copy_from_slice(&bytes[48..96]);\n\n        bls_fq_from_zcash_bytes(&tmp)?\n    };\n\n    let x = Fq2::new(xc0, xc1);\n\n    BlsG2Affine::get_point_from_x(x, flags.is_lexicographically_largest)\n}\n\n/// This serializes an Arkworks G2Affine point into a Zcash point encoding.\npub fn bls_g2_affine_to_zcash_bytes(p: &BlsG2Affine) -> [u8; G2_COMPRESSED_SIZE] {\n    let mut bytes = [0u8; G2_COMPRESSED_SIZE];\n\n    let c1_bytes = bls_fq_to_zcash_bytes(&p.x.c1);\n    let c0_bytes = bls_fq_to_zcash_bytes(&p.x.c0);\n    bytes[0..48].copy_from_slice(&c1_bytes[..]);\n    bytes[48..96].copy_from_slice(&c0_bytes[..]);\n\n    let encoding = EncodingFlags {\n        is_compressed: true,\n        is_infinity: p.infinity,\n        is_lexicographically_largest: p.y > -p.y,\n    };\n\n    encoding.encode_flags(&mut bytes[..]);\n    bytes\n}\n\n// Attempt to obtain x_coordinate\nfn obtain_x_coordinate(bytes: &[u8]) -> Option<Fq> {\n    let mut tmp = [0; G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE];\n    // this is safe as the private obtain_x_coordinate function is only invoked with G1 (48) and\n    // G2 (96) size inputs.\n    tmp.copy_from_slice(&bytes[0..G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE]);\n\n    // Mask away the flag bits\n    tmp[0] &= 0b0001_1111;\n\n    bls_fq_from_zcash_bytes(&tmp)\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\npub(crate) mod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use ark_bls12_381::{FqParameters, Fr as BlsFr};\n    use ark_ec::{AffineCurve, ProjectiveCurve};\n    use ark_ff::{Field, One, Zero};\n    use blst::{\n        blst_encode_to_g1, blst_encode_to_g2, blst_fp_from_uint64, blst_fr, blst_fr_from_uint64,\n        blst_p1, blst_p1_affine_compress, blst_p1_to_affine, blst_p1_uncompress, blst_p2,\n        blst_p2_affine_compress, blst_p2_to_affine, blst_p2_uncompress,\n    };\n    use proptest::{collection, prelude::*};\n\n    // Scalar roundtrips.\n\n    pub(crate) fn arb_bls_fr() -> impl Strategy<Value = BlsFr> {\n        collection::vec(any::<u8>(), 32..=32)\n            .prop_map(|bytes| BlsFr::from_random_bytes(&bytes[..]))\n            .prop_filter(\"Valid field elements\", Option::is_some)\n            .prop_map(|opt_fr| opt_fr.unwrap())\n            .no_shrink() // this is arbitrary.\n    }\n\n    fn arb_blst_fr() -> impl Strategy<Value = blst_fr> {\n        collection::vec(any::<u64>(), 4..=4)\n            .prop_map(|u64s| {\n                let mut out = blst_fr::default();\n                unsafe { blst_fr_from_uint64(&mut out, u64s[..].as_ptr()) };\n                out\n            })\n            .no_shrink() // this is arbitrary.\n    }\n\n    proptest! {\n        #[test]\n        fn Copyright (c) 2022, Mysten Labs, Inc.\n// SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0\n\nuse std::marker::PhantomData;\n\nuse ark_ff::PrimeField;\nuse ark_relations::{\n    lc,\n    r1cs::{\n        ConstraintSynthesizer, ConstraintSystemRef, LinearCombination, SynthesisError, Variable,\n    },\n};\n\n/// A dummy circuit that checks an addition constraint between some of its inputs\n/// as well as a parametrized number of dummy constraints.\n#[derive(Debug, Copy, Clone)]\npub struct DummyCircuit<F: PrimeField> {\n    /// the first input to the circuit\n    pub a: Option<F>,\n    /// the second input to the circuit\n    pub b: Option<F>,\n    /// The number of variables to the circuit\n    pub num_variables: usize,\n    /// the number of constraints to the circuit\n    pub num_constraints: usize,\n}\n\nimpl<F: PrimeField> ConstraintSynthesizer<F> for DummyCircuit<F> {\n    // We'll be proving a relationship involving the product c of a & b.\n    fn generate_constraints(self, cs: ConstraintSystemRef<F>) -> Result<(), SynthesisError> {\n        let a = cs.new_witness_variable(|| self.a.ok_or(SynthesisError::AssignmentMissing))?;\n        let b = cs.new_witness_variable(|| self.b.ok_or(SynthesisError::AssignmentMissing))?;\n        let c = cs.new_input_variable(|| {\n            let a = self.a.ok_or(SynthesisError::AssignmentMissing)?;\n            let b = self.b.ok_or(SynthesisError::AssignmentMissing)?;\n\n            Ok(a * b)\n        })?;\n\n        // a, b, c are above, let's define the rest.\n        for _ in 0..(self.num_variables - 3) {\n            let _ = cs.new_witness_variable(|| self.a.ok_or(SynthesisError::AssignmentMissing))?;\n        }\n\n        for _ in 0..self.num_constraints - 1 {\n            cs.enforce_constraint(lc!() + a, lc!() + b, lc!() + c)?;\n        }\n\n        cs.enforce_constraint(lc!(), lc!(), lc!())?;\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n\n/// A circuit that checks a parametrized number of R1CS constraints that verify the computation of the Fibonacci sequence.\n/// It generates constraints in layers, numbered from 1 to num_constraints, specifying the computation of the Fibonacci sequence.\n/// On each layer, it does:\n///\n/// 1. Add two new public inputs a = initial_a and b = initial_b at the first layer, or retrieve them from the previous layer if the layer if larger.\n/// 2. Add a new witness variable c,\n/// 3. Add and check a constraint that c = a + b,\n/// 4. Initialize the values a = b; b = c; and repeat from step 1.\n///\n/// Note: we encode the addition (c = a + b) less efficiently than what R1CS would allow, but this is not a problem for the purpose of this demo.\n#[derive(Debug)]\npub struct Fibonacci<F: PrimeField> {\n    num_constraints: usize,\n    initial_a: F,\n    initial_b: F,\n    _engine: PhantomData<F>,\n}\n\nimpl<F: PrimeField> Fibonacci<F> {\n    /// Create a new instance of the Fibonacci circuit. We have to provide the initial values of a and b, the first and second initial values of\n    /// a sequence verifying the Fibonacci recursion.\n    pub fn new(num_constraints: usize, initial_a: F, initial_b: F) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            num_constraints,\n            initial_a,\n            initial_b,\n            _engine: PhantomData,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl<F: PrimeField> ConstraintSynthesizer<F> for Fibonacci<F> {\n    fn generate_constraints(self, cs: ConstraintSystemRef<F>) -> Result<(), SynthesisError> {\n        let mut assignments = Vec::new();\n        let mut a_val = self.initial_a;\n        let mut a_var = cs.new_input_variable(|| Ok(a_val))?;\n        assignments.push((a_val, a_var));\n\n        let mut b_val = self.initial_b;\n        let mut b_var = cs.new_input_variable(|| Ok(b_val))?;\n        assignments.push((a_val, a_var));\n\n        for _i in 0..self.num_constraints - 1 {\n            let c_val = a_val + b_val;\n            let c_var = cs.new_witness_variable(|| Ok(c_val))?;\n\n            cs.enforce_constraint(lc!() + a_var + b_var, lc!() + Variable::One, lc!() + c_var)?;\n\n            assignments.push((c_val, c_var));\n            a_val = b_val;\n            a_var = b_var;\n            b_val = c_val;\n            b_var = c_var;\n        }\n\n        let mut a_lc = LinearCombination::zero();\n        let mut b_lc = LinearCombination::zero();\n        let mut c_val = F::zero();\n\n        for (val, var) in assignments {\n            a_lc = a_lc + var;\n            b_lc = b_lc + var;\n            c_val += val;\n        }\n        c_val = c_val.square();\n\n        let c_var = cs.new_witness_variable(|| Ok(c_val))?;\n\n        cs.enforce_constraint(lc!() + a_lc, lc!() + b_lc, lc!() + c_var)?;\n\n        Ok(())\n    }\n}\n","numLines":128,"startLine":1,"totalLines":128}}}

blst_fp { l: [0; 6] },\n154\t};\n155\t\n156\t/// Returns the log base 2 of b in O(lg(N)) time.\n157\tfn log_2_byte(b: u8) -> usize {\n158\t    let mut r = u8::from(b > 0xF) << 2;\n159\t    let mut b = pvk.alpha_g1_beta_g2)\n365\t}\n366\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"3bb9957a-b7f3-4aef-a7c2-70d073cac1ae","uuid":"57cb4952-1afb-47d9-89cc-9ce98e835df2","timestamp":"2026-07-02T06:56:24.625Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/app/fastcrypto-zkp/src/verifier.rs","content":"// Copyright (c) 2022, Mysten Labs, Inc.\n// SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0\nuse std::{iter, ops::Neg, ptr};\n\nuse ark_bls12_381::{Bls12_381, Fq12, Fr as BlsFr, G1Affine, G2Affine};\nuse ark_groth16::{Proof, VerifyingKey};\nuse ark_relations::r1cs::SynthesisError;\n\nuse ark_serialize::{CanonicalDeserialize, CanonicalSerialize};\nuse blst::{\n    blst_final_exp, blst_fp, blst_fp12, blst_fr, blst_miller_loop, blst_p1, blst_p1_add_or_double,\n    blst_p1_affine, blst_p1_from_affine, blst_p1_mult, blst_p1_to_affine, blst_p1s_mult_pippenger,\n    blst_p1s_mult_pippenger_scratch_sizeof, blst_scalar, blst_scalar_from_fr, limb_t, Pairing,\n};\nuse fastcrypto::error::FastCryptoError;\n\nuse crate::conversions::{\n    bls_fq12_to_blst_fp12, bls_fr_to_blst_fr, bls_g1_affine_to_blst_g1_affine,\n    bls_g2_affine_to_blst_g2_affine, blst_fp12_to_bls_fq12, G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE,\n};\n\n#[cfg(test)]\n#[path = \"unit_tests/verifier_tests.rs\"]\nmod verifier_tests;\n\n/// This is a helper function to store a pre-processed version of the verifying key.\n/// This is roughly homologous to [`ark_groth16::PreparedVerifyingKey`].\n/// Note that contrary to Arkworks, we don't store a \"prepared\" version of the gamma_g2_neg_pc,\n/// delta_g2_neg_pc fields, because we can't use them with blst's pairing engine.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct PreparedVerifyingKey {\n    /// The element vk.gamma_abc_g1,\n    /// aka the `[gamma^{-1} * (beta * a_i + alpha * b_i + c_i) * G]`, where i spans the public inputs\n    pub vk_gamma_abc_g1: Vec<G1Affine>,\n    /// The element `e(alpha * G, beta * H)` in `E::GT`.\n    pub alpha_g1_beta_g2: blst_fp12,\n    /// The element `- gamma * H` in `E::G2`, for use in pairings.\n    pub gamma_g2_neg_pc: G2Affine,\n    /// The element `- delta * H` in `E::G2`, for use in pairings.\n    pub delta_g2_neg_pc: G2Affine,\n}\n\nimpl PreparedVerifyingKey {\n    /// Deserialize the prepared verifying key from the serialized fields of vk_gamma_abc_g1, alpha_g1_beta_g2, gamma_g2_neg_pc, delta_g2_neg_pc\n    pub fn deserialize(\n        vk_gamma_abc_g1_bytes: &[u8],\n        alpha_g1_beta_g2_bytes: &[u8],\n        gamma_g2_neg_pc_bytes: &[u8],\n        delta_g2_neg_pc_bytes: &[u8],\n    ) -> Result<Self, FastCryptoError> {\n        let mut vk_gamma_abc_g1: Vec<G1Affine> = Vec::new();\n        for g1_bytes in vk_gamma_abc_g1_bytes.chunks(G1_COMPRESSED_SIZE) {\n            let g1 = G1Affine::deserialize(g1_bytes).map_err(|_| FastCryptoError::InvalidInput)?;\n            vk_gamma_abc_g1.push(g1);\n        }\n        let alpha_g1_beta_g2 = bls_fq12_to_blst_fp12(\n            &Fq12::deserialize(alpha_g1_beta_g2_bytes)\n                .map_err(|_| FastCryptoError::InvalidInput)?,\n        );\n\n        let gamma_g2_neg_pc = G2Affine::deserialize(gamma_g2_neg_pc_bytes)\n            .map_err(|_| FastCryptoError::InvalidInput)?;\n\n        let delta_g2_neg_pc = G2Affine::deserialize(delta_g2_neg_pc_bytes)\n            .map_err(|_| FastCryptoError::InvalidInput)?;\n\n        Ok(PreparedVerifyingKey {\n            vk_gamma_abc_g1,\n            alpha_g1_beta_g2,\n            gamma_g2_neg_pc,\n            delta_g2_neg_pc,\n        })\n    }\n\n    /// Serialize the prepared verifying key to its vectors form.\n    pub fn as_serialized(&self) -> Result<Vec<Vec<u8>>, FastCryptoError> {\n        let mut res = Vec::new();\n        let mut vk_gamma = Vec::new();\n        for g1 in &self.vk_gamma_abc_g1 {\n            let mut g1_bytes = Vec::new();\n            g1.serialize(&mut g1_bytes)\n                .map_err(|_| FastCryptoError::InvalidInput)?;\n            vk_gamma.append(&mut g1_bytes);\n        }\n        res.push(vk_gamma);\n        let mut fq12 = Vec::new();\n        blst_fp12_to_bls_fq12(&self.alpha_g1_beta_g2)\n            .serialize(&mut fq12)\n            .map_err(|_| FastCryptoError::InvalidInput)?;\n        res.push(fq12);\n\n        let mut gamma_bytes = Vec::new();\n        self.gamma_g2_neg_pc\n            .serialize(&mut gamma_bytes)\n            .map_err(|_| FastCryptoError::InvalidInput)?;\n        res.push(gamma_bytes);\n\n        let mut delta_bytes = Vec::new();\n        self.delta_g2_neg_pc\n            .serialize(&mut delta_bytes)\n            .map_err(|_| FastCryptoError::InvalidInput)?;\n        res.push(delta_bytes);\n        Ok(res)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Takes an input [`ark_groth16::VerifyingKey`] `vk` and returns a `PreparedVerifyingKey`. This is roughly homologous to\n/// [`ark_groth16::PreparedVerifyingKey::process_vk`], but uses a blst representation of the elements.\n///\n/// ## Example:\n/// ```\n/// use fastcrypto_zkp::{dummy_circuits::Fibonacci, verifier::process_vk_special};\n/// use ark_bls12_381::{Bls12_381, Fr};\n/// use ark_ff::One;\n/// use ark_groth16::{\n///     generate_random_parameters\n/// };\n/// use ark_std::rand::thread_rng;\n///\n/// let mut rng = thread_rng();\n/// let params = {\n///     let c = Fibonacci::<Fr>::new(42, Fr::one(), Fr::one()); // 42 constraints, initial a = b = 1 (standard Fibonacci)\n///     generate_random_parameters::<Bls12_381, _, _>(c, &mut rng).unwrap()\n/// };\n///\n/// // Prepare the verification key (for proof verification). Ideally, we would like to do this only\n/// // once per circuit.\n/// let pvk = process_vk_special(&params.vk);\n/// ```\npub fn process_vk_special(vk: &VerifyingKey<Bls12_381>) -> PreparedVerifyingKey {\n    let g1_alpha = bls_g1_affine_to_blst_g1_affine(&vk.alpha_g1);\n    let g2_beta = bls_g2_affine_to_blst_g2_affine(&vk.beta_g2);\n    let blst_alpha_g1_beta_g2 = {\n        let mut tmp = blst_fp12::default();\n        unsafe { blst_miller_loop(&mut tmp, &g2_beta, &g1_alpha) };\n\n        let mut out = blst_fp12::default();\n        unsafe { blst_final_exp(&mut out, &tmp) };\n        out\n    };\n    PreparedVerifyingKey {\n        vk_gamma_abc_g1: vk.gamma_abc_g1.clone(),\n        alpha_g1_beta_g2: blst_alpha_g1_beta_g2,\n        gamma_g2_neg_pc: vk.gamma_g2.neg(),\n        delta_g2_neg_pc: vk.delta_g2.neg(),\n    }\n}\n\n/// This helper constant makes it easier to use compute the linear combination involved in the pairing inputs.\nconst G1_IDENTITY: blst_p1 = blst_p1 {\n    x: blst_fp { l: [0; 6] },\n    y: blst_fp { l: [0; 6] },\n    z: blst_fp { l: [0; 6] },\n};\n\n/// Returns the log base 2 of b in O(lg(N)) time.\nfn log_2_byte(b: u8) -> usize {\n    let mut r = u8::from(b > 0xF) << 2;\n    let mut b = b >> r;\n    let shift = u8::from(b > 0x3) << 1;\n    b >>= shift + 1;\n    r |= shift | b;\n    r.into()\n}\n\n/// Returns a single scalar multiplication of `pt` by `b`.\nfn mul(pt: &blst_p1, b: &blst_fr) -> blst_p1 {\n    let mut scalar: blst_scalar = blst_scalar::default();\n    unsafe {\n        blst_scalar_from_fr(&mut scalar, b);\n    }\n\n    // Count the number of bytes to be multiplied.\n    let mut i = scalar.b.len();\n    while i != 0 && scalar.b[i - 1] == 0 {\n        i -= 1;\n    }\n\n    let mut result = blst_p1::default();\n    if i == 0 {\n        return G1_IDENTITY;\n    } else if i == 1 && scalar.b[0] == 1 {\n        return *pt;\n    } else {\n        // Count the number of bits to be multiplied.\n        unsafe {\n            blst_p1_mult(\n                &mut result,\n                pt,\n                &(scalar.b[0]),\n                8 * i - 7 + log_2_byte(scalar.b[i - 1]),\n            );\n        }\n    }\n    result\n}\n\n/// Facade for the blst_p1_add_or_double function.\nfn add_or_dbl(a: &blst_p1, b: &blst_p1) -> blst_p1 {\n    let mut ret = blst_p1::default();\n    unsafe {\n        blst_p1_add_or_double(&mut ret, a, b);\n    }\n    ret\n}\n\n/// Computes the [\\sum p_i * b_i, p_i in p_affine, b_i in coeffs] in G1.\nfn g1_linear_combination(\n    out: &mut blst_p1,\n    p_affine: &[blst_p1_affine],\n    coeffs: &[blst_fr],\n    len: usize,\n) {\n    if len < 8 {\n        // Direct approach\n        let mut tmp;\n        *out = G1_IDENTITY;\n        for i in 0..len {\n            let mut p = blst_p1::default();\n            unsafe { blst_p1_from_affine(&mut p, &p_affine[i]) };\n\n            tmp = mul(&p, &coeffs[i]);\n            *out = add_or_dbl(out, &tmp);\n        }\n    } else {\n        let mut scratch: Vec<u8>;\n        unsafe {\n            scratch = vec![0u8; blst_p1s_mult_pippenger_scratch_sizeof(len)];\n        }\n\n        let mut scalars = vec![blst_scalar::default(); len];\n\n        for i in 0..len {\n            let mut scalar: blst_scalar = blst_scalar::default();\n            unsafe {\n                blst_scalar_from_fr(&mut scalar, &coeffs[i]);\n            }\n            scalars[i] = scalar\n        }\n\n        let scalars_arg: [*const blst_scalar; 2] = [scalars.as_ptr(), ptr::null()];\n        let points_arg: [*const blst_p1_affine; 2] = [p_affine.as_ptr(), ptr::null()];\n        unsafe {\n            blst_p1s_mult_pippenger(\n                out,\n                points_arg.as_ptr(),\n                len,\n                scalars_arg.as_ptr() as *const *const u8,\n                256,\n                scratch.as_mut_ptr() as *mut limb_t,\n            );\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// This represents the multiplicative unit scalar, see fr_one_test\npub(crate) const BLST_FR_ONE: blst_fr = blst_fr {\n    l: [\n        8589934590,\n        6378425256633387010,\n        11064306276430008309,\n        1739710354780652911,\n    ],\n};\n\n/// Returns the result of the multi-pairing involved in the verification equation. This will then be compared to the pre-computed term\n/// pvk.alpha_g1_beta_g2 to check the validity of the proof.\n///\n/// The textbook Groth16 equation is (in additive notation):\n/// e(A, B) = e(g * alpha, h * beta) + e(g * f, h * gamma) + e(C, h * delta)\n/// where f is the linear combination of the a_i points in the verifying key with the input scalars\n///\n/// Due to the pre-processing of e(g * alpha, h * beta), and using the pairing inverse, we instead compute:\n/// e(A, B) + e(g * f, h * - gamma) + e(C, h * - delta).\n///\n/// Eventually, we will compare this value to  e(g * alpha, h * beta)\n///\nfn multipairing_with_processed_vk(\n    pvk: &PreparedVerifyingKey,\n    x: &[BlsFr],\n    proof: &Proof<Bls12_381>,\n) -> blst_fp12 {\n    // Linear combination: note that the arkworks interface assumes the 1st scalar is an implicit 1\n    let pts: Vec<blst_p1_affine> = pvk\n        .vk_gamma_abc_g1\n        .iter()\n        .map(bls_g1_affine_to_blst_g1_affine)\n        .collect();\n    let one = BLST_FR_ONE;\n    let ss: Vec<blst_fr> = iter::once(one)\n        .chain(x.iter().map(bls_fr_to_blst_fr))\n        .collect();\n    let mut out = blst_p1::default();\n    g1_linear_combination(&mut out, &pts, &ss[..], ss.len());\n\n    let blst_proof_a = bls_g1_affine_to_blst_g1_affine(&proof.a);\n    let blst_proof_b = bls_g2_affine_to_blst_g2_affine(&proof.b);\n\n    let mut blst_proof_1_g1 = blst_p1_affine::default();\n    unsafe { blst_p1_to_affine(&mut blst_proof_1_g1, &out) };\n    let blst_proof_1_g2 = bls_g2_affine_to_blst_g2_affine(&pvk.gamma_g2_neg_pc);\n\n    let blst_proof_2_g1 = bls_g1_affine_to_blst_g1_affine(&proof.c);\n    let blst_proof_2_g2 = bls_g2_affine_to_blst_g2_affine(&pvk.delta_g2_neg_pc);\n\n    let dst = [0u8; 3];\n    let mut pairing_blst = Pairing::new(false, &dst);\n    pairing_blst.raw_aggregate(&blst_proof_b, &blst_proof_a);\n    pairing_blst.raw_aggregate(&blst_proof_1_g2, &blst_proof_1_g1);\n    pairing_blst.raw_aggregate(&blst_proof_2_g2, &blst_proof_2_g1);\n    pairing_blst.as_fp12().final_exp()\n}\n\n/// Returns the validity of the Groth16 proof passed as argument. The format of the inputs is assumed to be in arkworks format.\n/// See [`multipairing_with_processed_vk`] for the actual pairing computation details.\n///\n/// ## Example\n/// ```\n/// use fastcrypto_zkp::{dummy_circuits::Fibonacci, verifier::{ process_vk_special, verify_with_processed_vk }};\n/// use ark_bls12_381::{Bls12_381, Fr};\n/// use ark_ff::One;\n/// use ark_groth16::{\n///     create_random_proof, generate_random_parameters\n/// };\n/// use ark_std::rand::thread_rng;\n///\n/// let mut rng = thread_rng();\n///\n/// let params = {\n///     let circuit = Fibonacci::<Fr>::new(42, Fr::one(), Fr::one()); // 42 constraints, initial a = b = 1\n///     generate_random_parameters::<Bls12_381, _, _>(circuit, &mut rng).unwrap()\n/// };\n///\n/// // Prepare the verification key (for proof verification). Ideally, we would like to do this only\n/// // once per circuit.\n/// let pvk = process_vk_special(&params.vk);\n///\n/// let proof = {\n///     let circuit = Fibonacci::<Fr>::new(42, Fr::one(), Fr::one()); // 42 constraints, initial a = b = 1\n///     // Create a proof with our parameters, picking a random witness assignment\n///     create_random_proof(circuit, &params, &mut rng).unwrap()\n/// };\n///\n/// // We provide the public inputs which we know are used in our circuits\n/// // this must be the same as the inputs used in the proof right above.\n/// let inputs: Vec<_> = [Fr::one(); 2].to_vec();\n///\n/// // Verify the proof\n/// let r = verify_with_processed_vk(&pvk, &inputs, &proof).unwrap();\n/// ```\n// TODO: due to arkworks incompatibilities in BLS12-381 point (de) serialization, we should probably implement a custom (de)serialization\n// for those formats, see https://github.com/arkworks-rs/algebra/issues/257\npub fn verify_with_processed_vk(\n    pvk: &PreparedVerifyingKey,\n    x: &[BlsFr],\n    proof: &Proof<Bls12_381>,\n) -> Result<bool, SynthesisError> {\n    // Note the \"+1\" : this API implies the first scalar coefficient is 1 and not sent\n    if (x.len() + 1) != pvk.vk_gamma_abc_g1.len() {\n        return Err(SynthesisError::MalformedVerifyingKey);\n    }\n\n    let res = multipairing_with_processed_vk(pvk, x, proof);\n    Ok(res == pvk.alpha_g1_beta_g2)\n}\n","numLines":366,"startLine":1,"totalLines":366}}}

at [`crate::verifier::PreparedVerifyingKey`]).\npub fn prepare_pvk_bytes(vk_bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Vec<Vec<u8>>, FastCryptoError> {\n    let vk = VerifyingKey::<Bls12_381>::deserialize(vk_bytes)\n        .map_err(|_| FastCryptoError::InvalidInput)?;\n\n    process_vk_special(&vk).as_serialized()\n}\n\n/// Verify Groth16 proof using the serialized form of the four components in a prepared verifying key\n/// (see more at [`crate::verifier::PreparedVerifyingKey`]), serialized proof public input and serialized proof points.\npub fn verify_groth16_in_bytes(\n    vk_gamma_abc_g1_bytes: &[u8],\n    alpha_g1_beta_g2_bytes: &[u8],\n    gamma_g2_neg_pc_bytes: &[u8],\n    delta_g2_neg_pc_bytes: &[u8],\n    proof_public_inputs_as_bytes: &[u8],\n    proof_points_as_bytes: &[u8],\n) -> Result<bool,
